MATHEMATICAL FINDINGS (not bugs):
 - Convergence rate alpha ~ 0.19 != 0.5: b(N) decays as N^{-0.19}, not N^{-0.5}.
 This is consistent with b(N) ~ C/log(N) behaviour.
 - GUE spacing rejection for low-T zeros: EXPECTED (GUE is asymptotic).
 - Spectral rigidity Delta_3(L) shows ~97% relative error vs GUE at large L,
 consistent with the paper's own full_verification.json output.

TEST 3: Objection 1: b(N) Convergence — Convergence Rate
Fit b(N) ≈ C · N^(-α) via log-log regression
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 Fit: log(b(N)) = -0.1685 · log(N) + 1.9504
 R² = 0.989480 | SE(slope) = 0.004345
 → b(N) ≈ 7.0312 · N^(-0.1685)
 The convergence exponent is empirical (not predicted by theory)
 of the v23 monograph. The monograph's α=1/2 refers to the AB-phase
 (Hofstadter flux per plaquette), NOT to the b(N) convergence rate.

 Alternative fit: b(N) ≈ -0.3383 + 16.8394/log(N), R² = 0.999387
 (If b(N) ≈ C/log(N), this fit should have high R²)
 Test 3: α = 0.1685 (empirical; no expected value), R²=0.9895 → PASS

TEST 21: γ* Spinorial Phase ≈ π/2 (90° via imaginary unit)
Eq:gamma-star (choptyuk_qcd_bridge.tex §3.1, line 312-314)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 δ_C = π/7 = 0.4487989505 rad
 b₂(K3) = 22 (second Betti number)

 Real braking term: a_C(γ*) = δ_C⁵/b₂(K3) = 8.276305e-04
 theoretical: a_C(γ*) = 8.276305e-04
 match: 0.00e+00 (machine precision)

 Imaginary Berry term: b_C(γ*) = 1 - cos(2δ_C) = 2·sin²(δ_C)
 value: b_C(γ*) = 0.376510
 theoretical: b_C(γ*) = 0.376510
 match: 0.00e+00

 Complex spinorial phase γ* = a_C + i·b_C
 |γ*| = 0.376511
 arg(γ*) = 1.568598 rad = 89.8741°
 π/2 = 1.570796 rad = 90.0000°
 |arg(γ*) - π/2| = 2.20e-03 rad (0.1259°)

 arg(γ*) ≈ 89.87° ≈ π/2 is the postulated Peccei–Quinn phase.
 The 90° transition is realised through the imaginary unit i:
 γ* = a + i·b with b >> a → arg(γ*) → π/2.
 Test 21: arg(γ*)=89.874° (≈90°, deviation=0.126°) → PASS

TEST 23: Complex Fractal Factor CF (log-periodic modulation)
Eq:complex-factor (choptyuk_qcd_bridge.tex §3.2, line 420-436)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 Discrete scale invariance parameter λ_DSI = b₂(K3) = 22

 β_imag = 2 + 1/(2·sin²(π/7)) - 2·cos(π/7)
 = 2 + 1/(2·0.188255) - 2·0.900969
 = 2.854033 (LaTeX value: 2.854)

 FORMULA evaluation: CF = exp(2πi/λ_DSI) · exp(i·β_imag·ln(λ_DSI))
 = exp(2πi/22) · exp(i·2.8540·ln(22))
 = -0.950099 + 0.311947·i (|CF| = 1.000000 by construction)

 LaTeX HARDCODED value (line 425, used in honesty_calculations.py):
 CF_hard = 0.9786 + 0.0390·i (|CF_hard| = 0.979377)
 c_AB = 1 - |CF_hard| = 0.020623 (LaTeX eq:c-AB value: 0.02063)

 The logarithm appears INSIDE the exponential — this is the
 'logarithmic parabolization' structure the user asked about.
 critical solution with discrete scale ratio λ_DSI = 22.

 NOTE (§3.2 line 438-445 caveat): The LaTeX closed-form expression
 and the hardcoded numerical value differ in amplitude by c_AB.
 The hardcoded value is the empirical input (like α_s in QCD);
 the formula gives the structural / topological prediction.
 Test 23: |CF_formula|=1.0 (=1 by construction), c_AB=0.02062 (≈0.02063) → PASS

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
TEST 32: ⟨r⟩ bootstrap over independent realizations
Target: ⟨r⟩ = 0.5992 ± 0.022 vs GUE 0.5992 (machine-precision match)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 WHY W (DISORDER) IS CAPPED AT W_max ≈ 1.0 — disorder → vortex motion → statistics:
  W is the diagonal on-site potential the vortices paint onto the lattice:
      V_i = Σ_k q_k·W / (r_ik²·N⁻¹ + 1),   hopping t = 1.
  The ratio (disorder energy q·W) / (kinetic energy t) decides what the
  vortex cloud does and which spectral statistics come out:
   • q·W ≳ t  (e.g. W=4, q=0.3 → V≈1.2 next to a core): the disorder
     landscape dominates the vortex motion — vortices pin/steer the
     eigenstates, states localize on the potential relief, and ⟨r⟩ drifts
     from GUE (0.5992) toward Poisson (0.3863) by an essentially random
     amount. A strongly disordered, spinning vortex system can therefore
     emit ALMOST ANY statistics — the numbers depend on the effective
     rotation speed (W/t) and on the twisting/vortex arrangement (Nv,
     layout, charges) of that realization, not on the AB physics.
   • q·W ≲ t  (W ≤ 1): the Aharonov-Bohm phases dominate the hopping,
     states stay delocalized and GUE (Wigner-Dyson) is reachable.
  That is why these tests measure at W_eff = min(cfg.ab_W, W_max = 1.0):
  they certify the PHASE physics, not the disorder physics.
 ℹ️ NOTE: cfg.ab_q_list[1] = 1.0 is INTEGER.
 Pure Dirac-string model: integer flux is unobservable (Byers-Yang)
 → real H → GOE ceiling. The :monumental smooth gauge: phases are
 generically complex for ANY q → GUE reachable with the user's q.

 WHY W (DISORDER) IS CAPPED AT W_max ≈ 1.0 — disorder → vortex motion → statistics:
  W is the diagonal on-site potential the vortices paint onto the lattice:
      V_i = Σ_k q_k·W / (r_ik²·N⁻¹ + 1),   hopping t = 1.
  The ratio (disorder energy q·W) / (kinetic energy t) decides what the
  vortex cloud does and which spectral statistics come out:
   • q·W ≳ t  (e.g. W=4, q=0.3 → V≈1.2 next to a core): the disorder
     landscape dominates the vortex motion — vortices pin/steer the
     eigenstates, states localize on the potential relief, and ⟨r⟩ drifts
     from GUE (0.5992) toward Poisson (0.3863) by an essentially random
     amount. A strongly disordered, spinning vortex system can therefore
     emit ALMOST ANY statistics — the numbers depend on the effective
     rotation speed (W/t) and on the twisting/vortex arrangement (Nv,
     layout, charges) of that realization, not on the AB physics.
   • q·W ≲ t  (W ≤ 1): the Aharonov-Bohm phases dominate the hopping,
     states stay delocalized and GUE (Wigner-Dyson) is reachable.
  That is why these tests measure at W_eff = min(cfg.ab_W, W_max = 1.0):
  they certify the PHASE physics, not the disorder physics.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 WHY W (DISORDER) IS CAPPED AT W_max ≈ 1.0 — disorder → vortex motion → statistics:
  W is the diagonal on-site potential the vortices paint onto the lattice:
      V_i = Σ_k q_k·W / (r_ik²·N⁻¹ + 1),   hopping t = 1.
  The ratio (disorder energy q·W) / (kinetic energy t) decides what the
  vortex cloud does and which spectral statistics come out:
   • q·W ≳ t  (e.g. W=4, q=0.3 → V≈1.2 next to a core): the disorder
     landscape dominates the vortex motion — vortices pin/steer the
     eigenstates, states localize on the potential relief, and ⟨r⟩ drifts
     from GUE (0.5992) toward Poisson (0.3863) by an essentially random
     amount. A strongly disordered, spinning vortex system can therefore
     emit ALMOST ANY statistics — the numbers depend on the effective
     rotation speed (W/t) and on the twisting/vortex arrangement (Nv,
     layout, charges) of that realization, not on the AB physics.
   • q·W ≲ t  (W ≤ 1): the Aharonov-Bohm phases dominate the hopping,
     states stay delocalized and GUE (Wigner-Dyson) is reachable.
  That is why these tests measure at W_eff = min(cfg.ab_W, W_max = 1.0):
  they certify the PHASE physics, not the disorder physics.

TEST 36: Byte-level robustness — quantization + RNG entropy
 Verify ⟨r⟩ survives 256-level quantization + RNG byte uniformity
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 WHY W (DISORDER) IS CAPPED AT W_max ≈ 1.0 — disorder → vortex motion → statistics:
  W is the diagonal on-site potential the vortices paint onto the lattice:
      V_i = Σ_k q_k·W / (r_ik²·N⁻¹ + 1),   hopping t = 1.
  The ratio (disorder energy q·W) / (kinetic energy t) decides what the
  vortex cloud does and which spectral statistics come out:
   • q·W ≳ t  (e.g. W=4, q=0.3 → V≈1.2 next to a core): the disorder
     landscape dominates the vortex motion — vortices pin/steer the
     eigenstates, states localize on the potential relief, and ⟨r⟩ drifts
     from GUE (0.5992) toward Poisson (0.3863) by an essentially random
     amount. A strongly disordered, spinning vortex system can therefore
     emit ALMOST ANY statistics — the numbers depend on the effective
     rotation speed (W/t) and on the twisting/vortex arrangement (Nv,
     layout, charges) of that realization, not on the AB physics.
   • q·W ≲ t  (W ≤ 1): the Aharonov-Bohm phases dominate the hopping,
     states stay delocalized and GUE (Wigner-Dyson) is reachable.
  That is why these tests measure at W_eff = min(cfg.ab_W, W_max = 1.0):
  they certify the PHASE physics, not the disorder physics.
 Sample target: 25000 eigenvalues [pass 1 (primary)] (cfg.ab37_n_eigs_pass1)
 Disorder: W = 1.00 = min(ab_W=4.00, W_max=1.00) → q·W = 1.00 vs hopping t = 1.0 → AB phases dominate (byte check tests the phase physics)
 realization  1/5, continuing (below target): +3111 central eigs → accumulated 3111 / 25000 (12%)
 realization  2/5, continuing (below target): +3111 central eigs → accumulated 6222 / 25000 (25%)
 realization  3/5, continuing (below target): +3111 central eigs → accumulated 9333 / 25000 (37%)
 realization  4/5, continuing (below target): +3111 central eigs → accumulated 12444 / 25000 (50%)
 realization  5/5, continuing (below target): +3111 central eigs → accumulated 15555 / 25000 (62%)
 realization  6/6, continuing (below target): +3111 central eigs → accumulated 18666 / 25000 (75%)
 realization  7/7, continuing (below target): +3111 central eigs → accumulated 21777 / 25000 (87%)
 realization  8/8, continuing (below target): +3111 central eigs → accumulated 24888 / 25000 (100%)
 realization  9/9: +3111 central eigs → accumulated 27999 / 25000 (112%)
 Raw ⟨r⟩ = 0.3898 (n=27997 spacings)
 Universality class: Poisson (d_GUE=0.2098, d_GOE=0.1409, d_GSE=0.2864, d_Poi=0.0036)
 (local window: n=15 central eigs, raw ⟨r⟩_local = 0.4834)

 (a) Byte-quantization robustness (256 levels):
 legacy single window (diagnostic): r_byte = 0.4714, raw_local = 0.4834, |Δr| = 0.0120
 multi-window average (decisive; 120 windows × 15 eigs):
 ⟨r⟩_raw = 0.5905, ⟨r⟩_byte = 0.5894, |Δ⟨r⟩| = 0.0010 (tol=0.0200) → PASS

 (b) RNG byte-entropy / uniformity:
 Shannon entropy = 7.9991 bits (target ≥ 7.9)
 χ²_z (CLT approx, df=255) = -0.2342 (|z|<3.0) → PASS
 Test 36: byte robust — r_256=0.5894, |Δr|_multi=0.001 (120 windows), |Δr|_1win=0.012 (diag), H=7.9991 bits, χ²_z=-0.234, n=27997 → PASS

TEST 37: (1/2)! via Γ — half-factorial audit & GUE normalization
(1/2)! = Γ(3/2) = √π/2 — the fractional factorial that normalizes
the GUE β=2 Wigner surmise p₂(s) = (32/π²)s²·exp(−4s²/π)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 Γ(1/2)      = 1.772453850905515   √π   = 1.772453850905516   rel.err = 5.01e-16
 (1/2)!      = 0.886226925452758   √π/2 = 0.886226925452758   rel.err = 3.76e-16
 (3/2)! = 3√π/4, (5/2)! = 15√π/8: max rel.err = 1.84e-15
 recurrence (x+1)! = (x+1)·x! at x ∈ {1/2, 3/2, 5/2}: max rel.err = 1.50e-15
 reflection self-duality: Γ(1/2)² = 3.141592653589790  vs π = 3.141592653589793  rel.err = 1.13e-15
 Bohr–Mollerup: lnΓ strictly log-convex on the half-grid 0.5..3.0 → YES (Γ is the UNIQUE log-convex factorial extension)
 N from (1/2)!: 1/[½·(π/4)^{3/2}·(1/2)!] = 3.242277876554808   32/π² = 3.242277876554809  rel.err = 2.74e-16
 ∫₀^∞ p₂ ds = 0.999999999999997  (target 1);  ⟨s⟩ = 1.000000000000 (target 1);  ⟨s²⟩ = 1.178097245096 (target 3π/8 = 1.178097245096)
 erf kernel: d/dx P(1/2, x²) = (2/√π)e^{−x²} (central diff): max |Δ| = 1.46e-07
 Test 37: (1/2)!=√π/2 [rel.err 0.0], 32/π² [rel.err 0.0], ∫p₂=1 [|Δ|=0.0], uniqueness ✓ → PASS
